Due to that penance, it is about to receive Murthiyar, who performed penance, as its king. Such a glorious Murthi Nayanar emerges from the Thiru Aalavaai temple and stands there. At that moment, the royal elephant that arrived there lowered its head to the ground, bowed to Murthi Nayanar, with the jewels embedded in its golden forehead touching the ground, lifted Murthi Nayanar, and immediately placed him on its back. At that time, the ministers bowed at Murthi Nayanar's sacred feet and rose. It is customary for ministers to bow when a king is present and for it to be the first act of governance. All the ministers came there, bowed, and worshipped Murthi Nayanar. "All the ministers" indicates ministers who manage and execute various departments. These departments include justice, city security, royal revenue, and so on. It was also customary in those days for one of them to be the chief minister. As soon as the elephant lifted Murthi Nayanar onto its back and all the ministers bowed, the people of the town rejoiced with great cheers. They played various musical instruments. Everyone celebrated Murthi Nayanar, who had received the grace of the Lord who has Angayarkanni Ammaiyar as one part of Himself. Trumpets and conches blared. Kettledrums boomed. Various instruments sounded. All these spread and proclaimed the auspicious news for the townspeople to know. It was customary in those days to proclaim festivals and other events by sounding kettledrums and other instruments. Thus, they dismounted Murthi Nayanar, who was carried by the elephant, from the royal elephant, placed him on the beautiful platform of the coronation hall, seated him on the throne, and began to arrange the rituals for his coronation, as he was about to assume kingship under the unique moon-like umbrella. In the direction where Murthi Nayanar was seated, in the altar set up opposite him (i.e., in the altar set up in the east), they kindled fire in the sacrificial pit with Shiva mantras. "Mangala Aahuthi" means an offering that brings auspiciousness. Shiva is auspiciousness. Lord Shiva is the source and cause of all auspiciousness. Not only that, He is also the prime mover of all sacrifices. Therefore, Shiva sacrifices are mentioned here. The Agamic rule of sacrificers taking the sun's rays with a sunstone and kindling it to begin the sacrifice is mentioned here. Thus, they kindled the sacrificial fire with that radiant flame. They consecrated the kalasams (pots). "Thuthaindha nool soozh ponnin kalasangal" (golden kalasams tightly wound with thread) appears here. The thread wound around the kalasam is considered one of its seven components. It is not permissible to consecrate a kalasam without winding thread around it. Therefore, "tightly winding the thread" means winding it closely with three strands of thread as prescribed. Thus, they consecrated golden kalasams, wound with thread as prescribed, in the center of the altar. These kalasams are the primary Shiva Shakti kalasams. These are called principal kalasams, and others are called subsidiary kalasams. It is customary to consecrate and worship the respective deities in them with appropriate mantras, perform homam, and then bathe the king with the water from these kalasams. The water placed in the kalasam is pure water. The purity of that water comes from pure substances like ant-hill earth, sea earth, mountain-base earth, jewels, etc., placed within it, and also from mantras and bhavana (meditation). Thus, the Jnana Desikas (spiritual teachers) who were capable of making the intended outcome happen according to prescribed rules through mantra, bhavana, and kriya, performed those rituals. All these are rituals prescribed in the Saiva Agamas. Murthi Nayanar, who always kept Lord Shiva in his thoughts and attained clarity, looked at the people who had come and bowed to him, and said, "If the former Jainism departs and the primordial Saivism flourishes, then I will rule this world and perform a just kingship." Here, Sekkizhar Peruman refers to Murthi Nayanar as "Chinthai Shivame Theliyum Thiru Murthiyar" (The sacred Murthiyar whose mind clearly perceives Shiva). Murthi Nayanar, who clearly understood Shiva alone as the truth in his mind. This should be interpreted as "Chinthai Shivame Theliyum" (mind that clearly perceives Shiva). The particle "e" in "Shivame" is an emphatic particle. It means that he thought of nothing else but Shiva's feet. Because he was so clear, he said, "If Jainism departs and Saivism flourishes, I will rule." Otherwise, he would not have valued kingship. Among our great Nayanmar devotees, there are two Nayanmars who are in the ascetic state, the Sannyasa ashrama. One of them is our Murthi Nayanar. Therefore, it is implied that he renounced all royal regalia like crown, golden ornaments, golden robes, etc. Thus, Murthiyar, the king of ascetics, who had renounced the world, explains the reason for his agreeing to be the king of the earth. The reason Murthi Nayanar agreed to accept kingship was his zeal for the advancement of Saivism. He did not have any attachment to kingship but subjugated it to the service of Lord Shiva, stating that he would accept kingship only for the sake of Lord Shiva's service. Not only that, but he also stated that previous practices like Jainism should cease. He said that Jainism, which had come some time ago, should disappear. This is because the forceful rule of Jains was not ordained by the all-knowing Lord but was done by ignorant humans. Furthermore, he refers to Saivism as "Muthal Saivam" (primordial Saivism). This means the primary religion revealed by Lord Shiva, the foremost. It also means being the first religion among all religions. It also means Saivism, which encompasses all other religions as its parts. When Murthi Nayanar said this, the ministers and those highly learned in true scriptures bowed to Murthi Nayanar and said, "O great one, whatever you command, we will do. What else can we do?" They prostrated at Murthi Nayanar's feet. We will continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 11 — Murthi Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Periya Puranam, Part 190, Mummaiyaal Ulaganda Sarukkam, Murthi Nayanar Puranam. Murthi Nayanar states that he will accept kingship if Jainism departs and primordial Saivism flourishes. The ministers agree to this. After that, Murthi Nayanar says, "If I am to rule this world, then the sacred ash that shines on Lord Shiva's body must be my coronation anointing, the Rudraksha, which is Lord Shiva's symbol, must be my precious ornaments, and the matted hair, which is the Lord's sacred symbol, must be my royal crown." Murthi Nayanar begins by saying, "Vayyam murai seigavan aagil" (If I am to rule the world justly). "Vayyam murai seithal" means to govern. Its meaning is to establish the people of the world in their appropriate states as prescribed by the laws of justice. This is the primary duty of kings. If he is to govern, Murthi Nayanar says that the sacred ash that shines on Lord Shiva's body must be his coronation anointing. This sacred ash is the one that bestows greatness upon all who wear it with devotion. The Nayanar says that he will accept such sacred ash as his anointing. "Abhishekam" means ritual bathing. It is customary to pour sacred water for coronation. Therefore, it means ritual bathing. It means ritual bathing performed for coronation. The mantra yogis had arranged pure water in golden kalasams and pots for the ritual bathing. But Murthiyar considered the application of sacred ash as his anointing, above that pure water. To bathe with sacred ash, to consider sacred ash as the anointing substance, means to apply sacred ash profusely. Because it purifies more than bathing in water, applying sacred ash is specially referred to as Bhasma Snanam (ash bath), Agneya Snanam (fire bath), bathing in ash, and bathing in fire, in Saiva tradition. Regarding this, elders say, "After bathing in water, bathe in sacred ash." The Nayanar says that he will accept this sacred ash as his anointing substance. Now, he says that he will accept the Rudraksha garland, which is Lord Shiva's sacred symbol, as his ornament. Rudraksha is said to have originated from the compassionate tears of Lord Rudra's eyes. Therefore, it is also called "kanmani" (eye-gem). Just as a woman wearing a thali (mangalsutra) is a symbol of her being married to a husband, so too a soul should wear Rudraksha as a symbol of its belonging to Lord Shiva, its soul's beloved, according to Saiva tradition and rules. It is also implied that people who do not wear Rudraksha are like women who do not know their husband. It is also known from the divine utterance "Poopatharkku kandiyinai koosi idum pulliyarai kaanpatharkku koosum aran kaiththu" that Lord Shiva Himself would hesitate to see people who do not wear Rudraksha. Murthi Nayanar says that sacred ash should be the anointing, the Rudraksha, which is Lord Shiva's symbol, should be the ornaments, and matted hair should be the crown. Matted hair is also called "Jatabharam." Its meaning is to bear the weight of matted hair. This matted hair is a special symbol unique to Lord Shiva. The Nayanar says that this matted hair should be the jeweled crown worn by kings. Murthiyar, who held onto the feet of his Lord, who protects the entire world, took the divine symbols of His sovereignty as the symbols of his own worldly kingship. Murthiyar says, "Maamudiye mudiyavathu" (The great matted hair itself is the crown). Therefore, it is implied that he was already a great ascetic with long matted hair. The three mentioned here – sacred ash, Rudraksha, and matted hair – are called Shiva Sadhanas (symbols of Shiva). Murthiyar, who previously wore these and shone as a great devotee of Shiva, even when he became a great emperor ruling the world, did not change but adopted these very same things as symbols for that as well. His devotion is worthy of worship. We must also consider the ideas that "those who have transcended both destruction and creation, who view gold and copper equally" and "those who worship the feet of the universal cause are worthy to rule all worlds." Hearing Murthi Nayanar's words, the ministers, who possessed immeasurable knowledge, firm intellect, scriptural wisdom, and growing truthfulness, bowed to Murthi Nayanar and said that what he had commanded was good. Then, to suit the mind of Murthi Nayanar, the king of ascetics, they performed all the rituals pertaining to his right to rule. Various auspicious musical instruments played. The sounds of people's blessings were heard everywhere. Amidst all these sounds, Murthi Nayanar, who had assumed kingship, went to the Thiru Aalavaai temple and bowed low to Lord Sokkalinga enshrined there. The first act Murthi Nayanar did after being crowned king was to go and worship Lord Aalavaai. This was due to the immense compassion the Lord had shown him earlier, and also to seek His divine grace to assist and guide his future reign. It is the duty of worldly people, who become deluded and arrogant even with a little wealth, forget Lord Shiva, and commit many sins, to understand this and attain salvation. Sekkizhar Peruman says, "Sendru thaazhndhu Thiru Aalavaai sendru thaazhndhu" (went and bowed, went to Thiru Aalavaai and bowed), referring to Murthi Nayanar going to the Thiru Aalavaai temple after his coronation. When describing his departure from the temple after worshipping the Lord there, Sekkizhar Peruman says, "Murthi Nayanar kalitrin misai ponthaar" (Murthi Nayanar went on an elephant). Therefore, it is understood that Murthi Nayanar walked without a vehicle when going from the coronation hall to worship at the Thiru Aalavaai temple, and after worshipping the Lord in the temple, he mounted an elephant and went on a procession through the streets. The rule that one should not go in a vehicle when going for darshan at Lord Shiva's temple is derived here. Thus, Murthi Nayanar departs from the Thiru Aalavaai temple, mounts an elephant, and circumambulates the sacred streets of Thiru Aalavaai. The sacred streets of Thiru Aalavaai are the sacred streets that extend due to the divine grace of Lord Sokkalinga, the sacred deity of Thiru Aalavaai, who performed many divine sports. It is said that there are no streets in this city of Madurai where Lord Shiva, who performed 64 divine sports, did not set His divine foot, to the extent that the sacred streets of Madurai are filled with divine grace. For some reason or another, the Lord performs divine sports in all the sacred streets here. Thus, Murthi Nayanar, who went on a procession through the sacred streets, goes to the royal palace. There, he reaches the hall where the royal court is held and sits on the throne, the royal seat supported by lions and adorned with gold. Fly-whisks are waved on both sides. He rules from that throne under the white royal umbrella. Ministers, who administer justice in various departments, act according to Murthi Nayanar's indications. The control of the rebellious Jains was removed. The rebellion caused by Jains in Madurai was not only their following their own religion but also their causing trouble to other religions. Such rebellion caused by them was stopped there, and all the troubles arising from it were removed. Just as a soul flourishes only when its bonds of attachment are removed, so too, when the Jain control that prevented Saivism from flourishing in Madurai was removed, all the souls living there turned towards Saivism. Sekkizhar Swamigal states, "Nilavum Thiruneetru Nerithurai Needu Vaazha Ulagengum Nirambiya Saivam Uyarndhu Manna" (May the path of sacred ash flourish forever, and may Saivism, which pervades the world, rise high and endure). The path of sacred ash is the path of Shiva. It is praised in various ways as the path of light, the great path, the sacred path, the path of grace, in Tamil scriptures like Thevaram and Thiruvasagam. The Pandya country became a place where such a path existed, and where activities like entering, staying, and residing in that path of sacred ash took place. Saivism, which pervaded the world, became firmly established. What does it mean that the state of Saivism pervades the entire world? It means that the symbols of Shiva Shakti become the entire world. The truth that the symbols of Shiva Shakti become the entire world is known from the Thirugnanasiddhiyar verse beginning "Sathiyum Shivamumaay thanmai ivvulagamellaam oththavvaa aanum pennum uyar guna guniyumaagi," and from the Thirugnanasiddhiyar verse "Yaathorutheivam kondir attheivamagi ange maathorubaganar thaam varuvar," and from Appar Swamigal's Thiruvaiaatru Thiruppathigam "Kaathan madappidiyodum kaliru varuvana kanden kanden avar thiruppatham kandariyathana kanden," and from the Thirunavukkarasar Puranam verse "Sathiyum Shivamumaam sarithai panmai yonigal yaavaiyum payilvana panindhe," and from the Vedas.
